## Current state — 4 October 2026

The tables below supersede earlier sequencing and status summaries. The detailed historical evidence remains below; its former worktree paths and “next task” directions are historical, not execution instructions.

Physical release, stock activation, other accounting treatments and all retained M1–M6 scope remain required. Source publication is distinct from deployment; staging and production remain unaccepted.

### M4 mixed-receipt costing — implementation in progress

The current work connects explicitly approved M77 quantities to M70 invoice matching and M73 partial valuation. Invoice matching selects a particular quality allocation and retains authenticated receipt/decision/lot facts. Existing inspection-head serialization must cap all matches against that allocation; existing receipt and match valuation capacities continue to cap quantities and exact net/tax/charge amounts. Clean v1 snapshots remain unchanged. Additive migration78, the scoped selector, supplier-claim provenance and the combined browser workflow are being implemented; none is yet locally validated or production accepted. Goods remain held throughout this slice.

### M4 physical quality acceptance — locally verified and published

Published on GitHub `main` at `8983694f709417a2d36a82bee6faec3120c9560c`. GitHub confirms tree `b242151249e9c24f710ac04c507be1fd8cba9f32` and parent `6201a8d87103e8239bdf9d6622069dcae5cb8de6`; [focused CI](https://github.com/natannielz/Capstone/actions/runs/37173013291) completed successfully for that exact source. Local source/build/outcome provenance is recorded in `tmp/production/ocr-results/quality-acceptance-checkpoint-release1.json`, and remote identity in `quality-acceptance-publication-release1.json`. Publication did not deploy or activate production.

The M77 candidate adds explicit, independently observed quality acceptance for one genuine held receipt. Maker and checker must agree on the segregated quantity, lot, putaway, packaging condition and expiry facts. Mixed inspection observations are never added or subtracted to infer good stock. Sealed/loose conditions come from the retained package definition. A dated lot must retain its original future expiry; a genuinely non-expiring lot needs a finite approved policy and an independently agreed reason. Approval records `approved_held` capacity without changing stock, availability, money or M73 valuation. Partial valuation, actual source activation and physical release remain separate required work.

Backend `quality-acceptance-release14` builds without warnings or errors. Frontend production build `KGheuZC68UkvGOs1Eka_P`, focused frontend checks, TypeScript, changed-file lint and module boundaries pass. The compiled API contract export and generated-client check pass (`quality-acceptance-contract-check-release3`). Product assemblies are byte-identical from backend release9 through release14. Later changes affect only test fixtures/assertions and the browser driver; frontend product source is unchanged from its verified production build.

`quality-acceptance-wire-authority-release2` passes strict input, scoped permissions and Auth0 operation/return-path checks on release9. `quality-acceptance-core-release2` passes the complete database batch on release10: genuine mixed-subset acceptance, exact original-key recovery and privacy, broken-owner and participant rejection, competing accepted-quantity commands, dated/non-expiring rules and final-write authority. The strengthened authority case passes separately in the release11 integration batch: expiry uses a separate short-lived immutable policy, and retained rejection remains readable. That batch also passes empty upgrade and unsafe-storage refusal. The older M76 predecessor-isolation compatibility case passes in `quality-acceptance-migration-browser-release2` on release13. These named successes do not make either mixed-result batch successful overall.

Earlier build, permission-wiring and test failures remain under their original artifact names. Product corrections carry the stored maker as `RequestedBy`, supply the exact maker/checker subset to the shared person predicate, preserve the full policy/person fences, and carry the same approval through command-target authorization and retained person-receipt verification. `quality-acceptance-core-release1` was explicitly aborted after known failures and supplies no aggregate acceptance. Golden release4 exposed a test expectation for a nullable missing-proposal read; the corrected regression requires unavailable detail and denial of both new acceptance and retained-result recovery when history is broken.

The release11 integration batch and release13 migration/browser batch are retained as failed. Exact diagnostics identified both retained migration differences as the legitimate allocator floor for a directly inserted synthetic supplier policy (ID7001). Successful-upgrade expected snapshots now derive that specific counter from retained policy IDs; refusal snapshots and every other counter, business row, definition, UUID and index remain exact. Release11's browser reached genuine acceptance and lost-response recovery but its synthetic reader incorrectly had broad source-read grants. Release13 narrowed it to quality read, then hit native datetime-local normalization of `.920` to `.92` in Playwright field entry. The corrected driver keeps ordinary field entry and verifies the exact original instant; an isolated browser probe preserves all tested precision/rollover values. `quality-acceptance-retained-browser-release3` passes the complete corrected cohort on release14: both retained migration cases and the browser journey. Mobile/desktop review screenshots from release11 were inspected and preserved under `tmp/production/invoice-browser-results/quality-acceptance-release11`; the passing run also captures both layouts.

The passing real-browser journey takes 166 seconds against the production Next build, local HTTPS API, isolated MongoDB and private synthetic evidence. It proves two actual OIDC step-up rotations, independently entered observations, lost committed-response recovery with the original key and no resend, wrong-actor recovery denial, physical-only history, denial of source reads to a quality-only reader and foreign-scope denial. Exactly one package is accepted while all three receipt packages remain held; stock, money and valuation do not change. This is local integration, not real-provider or production acceptance. The repeated M72 source proofs and multi-second local reads/writes remain measured performance work before release; any reuse must retain transaction identity, live capacity, source, policy and person fences.

Quality-only readers receive a physical receipt projection without supplier prices, payment terms or unrelated order lines. New source review still requires procurement and stock read authority. Rejection can close stale observations/policy and revoked maker grants, but retains active original identity and verified-person requirements; inactive-maker task resolution remains part of M5. Coordination-only pool fences are excluded from preview fingerprints while actual source configuration and current transaction fences remain checked. Mixed-receipt costing must next connect both M70 invoice matching and M73 valuation to authenticated accepted quantities: both currently require clean inspection observations. Existing clean historical snapshots and the receipt/match capacity guards must remain intact. See the [operator runbook](../runbooks/procurement-quality-acceptance.md). No staging or production acceptance is claimed.

### M4 returned supplier advances — verified and published checkpoint

The browser journey took 272.354 seconds within its unchanged 480-second bound. `advance-return-core-release1.trx` passes its complete frozen release8 run. Genuine customer verification and supplier return can commit only one permanent receipt owner; full customer unallocation does not release its purpose; supplier-owned receipts leave the customer picker while generic authorized bank history remains readable. Shared advance application versus return admits only one competing over-capacity action, preserves legacy snapshots and permits only the actual remainder afterward. Genuine foreign supplier/order/book combinations are refused, prior payable owners cannot reopen capacity, zero-advance recognition remains independently authorized, and competing payout recordings cannot overdraw funding or reuse a draft. Current admission/early-scope, retained-migration and affected browser checks pass on release9. The reviewed source is now published; full release/provider/pilot acceptance remains pending.

The M76 candidate implements the user's whole-credit boundary: one verified incoming bank receipt belongs permanently to one supplier and one purchase order, with explicit amounts against one or more genuine verified advance payouts from that order. Independent approval debits the original receipt suspense account and credits supplier advance. It neither repeats cash nor reduces supplier-payable debt. Application and return consume the same per-advance balance and transaction sequence; legacy v1 snapshots remain exact, and the first return introduces an explicit v2 state with applied, returned and remaining amounts. See the [operator runbook](../runbooks/supplier-advance-returns.md).

Customer verification and supplier return contend on the existing unique receipt-purpose owner. Customer selection now uses its genuine declaration and current bank/account authority to exclude supplier-owned or other-payer receipts; generic bank history remains readable. Missing decisions, lines, purposes, heads and retained command evidence fail closed. The orphan-journal guard is bounded to the authenticated receipt's accounting book. Because a journal retains its source-owner ID rather than a receipt ID, an orphan conservatively prevents new attribution within that book until repaired; this is a known operational/performance limit, not a cross-book lock.

Backend `advance-return-release9` builds with zero warnings/errors; its captured source path set, source hashes and compiled assembly hashes match throughout final validation. It includes the shared-storage admission and early-scope fixes. Release8's completed money-race and pin/whole-credit evidence remains explicitly tied to release8. Earlier compile failures remain preserved as releases1–4; release5 compiled but its customer-picker test changed afterward, release6 reached the source-reading failure below, and release7 exposed the short-pin repeated-proof issue. Domain, native DTO serialization, explicit permissions and exact Auth0 return-path checks pass in the final release9 admission/authority run. Focused frontend checks pass on current source: `advance-return-frontend-focused-release2`, `advance-return-payable-frontend-focused-release1` and `advance-return-customer-frontend-focused-release1`. The first publication admission stopped before staging because the earlier return-only focused snapshot predated the final browser harness and generated API contract; that one affected check was rerun successfully with unchanged source during execution, preserving the original evidence. Type checking, changed-file lint and module boundaries pass. Frontend build `advance-return-frontend-release1`, ID `MJYRTZz0WfZn8mVtwV1J8`, passes and remains unchanged; compiled-contract and generated-client checks also pass from unchanged API/contract sources.

The admission review found that M75 public operations required its extended advance validator but did not require M76 return collections/indexes and the shared v2 receipt-purpose validator. Missing return storage could therefore appear as empty history. The correction reuses the exact current M76 storage gate from M75 before capacity reconstruction; direct internal migration75 preflight remains separate and valid before76. The genuine whole-credit case passes on release9 with the added storage regression: it temporarily removes/restores an empty required return collection/index and reverts/restores purpose validation, requiring current advance reads, recognition preview and commands to fail without effects. Restored storage then supports the genuine multi-payout return and later payable application. The complete `advance-return-admission-authority-release1.trx` run passes, including native domain/DTO/permissions/Auth0 checks; source and assembly hashes still match release9 afterward.

The direct detail reader now checks the stored proposal scope before traversing historical owners, while retaining the complete owner proof, canonical scope check and final authorization fence. The release9 authority regression passes: with a genuine downstream settlement temporarily removed, foreign scope receives a permission denial first, whereas an authorized reader detects the missing proof. Current people/grants, final-write MFA and policy expiry, rollback and rejection also pass. Release8 evidence remains explicitly tied to its original compiled candidate; the affected current source is validated by release9.

`advance-return-migrations-release1.trx` passes the complete M75/M76 retained-history run on release9. Empty and resumed upgrades preserve stronger validators without creating financial owners. Genuine valuation, private supplier invoices, verified bank advances, v1 payable applications and v1 customer receipt purposes/private submissions survive their additive upgrades unchanged. Missing valuation/journal/bank/private-pin proof refuses before DDL. A completed supplier return cannot be treated as an empty predecessor. Source and compiled hashes remain unchanged afterward. These are isolated local upgrade checks; coordinated production migration, backup/restore and operator acceptance remain M6 gates.

Physical release remains a distinct M4 gap. Inspection records overlapping condition observations and optional lot/expiry/putaway details, not an approved releasable subset. M72 proves the original fully held receipt, and M74 independently assumes that initial balance and one receipt movement; both need authenticated subsequent movement history before a release can be valid. Valuation can bound cost-backed quantity but cannot establish quality. The existing release transition increases available quantity and requires an active pool, so any quality approval while the source is stopped must leave quantity held until an authorized activation/release transition. Receipt-versus-claim physical allocation remains separate from release capacity.

The first genuine whole-credit database run (`advance-return-whole-credit-release1`) failed before recording a return: the source reader used the compact supplier view's ID field directly on the retained raw supplier snapshot. Release7 corrects this through the owning supplier projection. It also explicitly binds a reused return-line proof's proposal ID as well as its hash, with a corruption regression using another genuine proposal, and rechecks recovery authority after historical verification. Valid bank reads update a coordination fence, so retained-source assertions normalize only that field while preserving every immutable receipt and journal fact. The second whole-credit run (`advance-return-whole-credit-release2`, release7) failed earlier, while creating the second genuine advance: the existing private-evidence pin expired at its final check. That setup failure supplies no return-workflow acceptance. Release8 corrects repeated historical proof work while retaining the five-second lease and security guards; the complete genuine two-advance return and changed-prior-claim rollback pass in `advance-return-pin-and-whole-credit-release1.trx`. Observed final pin lease elapsed times were about 4.22 and 4.27 seconds, with readable originals; this is not production load acceptance. The earlier single-payout authority run passes on release7; the expanded current authority case passes on release9. The complete concurrency, customer-purpose, retained-migration and affected browser outcomes are recorded above. Transaction-local historical owner reuse remains pinned to the exact session/transaction and immutable owner facts, with individual line, balance, reverse-effect, purpose and authorization proofs still required. Source publication, production performance, actual Finance policy/mappings, provider deployment and all remaining M4–M6 scope remain separate.

### M4 supplier payables and advance application — verified local checkpoint

M76 validation follow-up: the second genuine payout exposed repeated verification of earlier payout history inside the existing short evidence-pin window. The correction reuses earlier fully proven immutable claims only within the same transaction and funding source, comparing current stored claim bytes and rebuilding allocation/head sequences before the unchanged final evidence and authority fences. A regression mutates an earlier claim inside that transaction to require rollback. `advance-return-pin-and-whole-credit-release1.trx` passes on frozen release8: the changed prior claim is rejected after real pin creation, its mutation and new effects roll back, and the unchanged second payout succeeds under the original evidence limits. The whole `7000.00` credit returns `4000.00` and `3000.00` against two actual same-order advances, preserves original cash/debt, permanently owns the receipt, rejects missing/relinked historical proof and recovers exact results. A subsequent `1000.00` payable application leaves `1000.00` on the first advance and `10250.00` supplier debt. Full final validation and its candidate-specific evidence are recorded in the M76 section above. The published M75 evidence below remains historical evidence for its own checkpoint.

Published normally to GitHub main at `4eeb1276f02480c8dcaa1b0d16a939deaac09300`; the remote commit, tree and parent match the reviewed checkpoint. Current branch rules permitted the direct push. Only the explicitly preserved unfinished return input/test files remained outside the commit at publication; subsequent return implementation continues in the same authoritative checkout. Exact [focused CI 37151982369](https://github.com/natannielz/Capstone/actions/runs/37151982369) completed successfully. Source/publication proofs are `tmp/production/ocr-results/supplier-payable-checkpoint-release1.json` and `supplier-payable-publication-release1.json`. This is source publication, not deployment acceptance.

Two explicit reviewed transitions use existing genuine sources: recognition from an approved held-receipt valuation allocation, and a bounded application from an independently bank-verified supplier advance to that recognized payable. Focused native checks, local migration and the complete browser journey pass with independent, reconstructable balances; a zero-advance order can retain unpaid debt. Source cash movement is not repeated, and unused-advance return requires a separate genuine bank-credit workflow. See the [operator runbook](../runbooks/supplier-payables.md). Real Finance policy/account mappings, conflict policy, all remaining F06 outcomes and production acceptance remain open.

The existing accounting setup is explicitly initial-only and allows one approved configuration per book key. A book missing the required supplier-payable mappings cannot be silently amended or replaced with a second approval. The connected local fixture must establish a genuinely approved complete initial chart before any posting; reviewed chart/setup revision remains separate required M4 work. Historical sources are matched by their actual book and posting-account identities, not by invented policy renewal.

User decision, 4 October 2026: each returned supplier-advance bank credit must belong entirely to one supplier and one purchase order. Mixed customer/supplier or multi-order credits are not an accepted input. The return workflow must enforce exclusive receipt ownership together with the shared remaining advance, so later customer verification/allocation cannot consume the same money. This defines the requested implementation boundary; actual Finance policy/account mappings and real bank evidence still require operational acceptance.

Native integrity checks pass. `supplier-payable-core-release2.trx` contains passing zero-advance, current-maker revocation, independent approval, final-write policy/MFA expiry rollback and existing scope checks. Its stale Finance-suggestion expectation is corrected and passes in `supplier-payable-core-release3.trx`, together with explicit payable authority and exact Auth0 return-path checks. The third browser run successfully renewed both real local OIDC sessions and committed both approvals, but application recovery correctly remained pending: the API persisted and returned a null payable ID for an approved application. The correction stores the original verified payable ID and requires it to agree with both the proposal and application when reading retained records. Client recovery remains strict. `supplier-payable-core-release5.trx` passes the complete genuine shared-advance case with exact result, recovery, read and wire IDs plus null/foreign retained-owner rejection; current authority, scope, Auth0 and domain checks also pass.

Backend `supplier-payable-release8`, production frontend `supplier-payable-frontend-release5` (build ID `-14bZh_oOgcrQkI1fq9xB`), compiled/generated contracts, changed-file lint, module boundaries and focused frontend `supplier-payable-frontend-focused-release6` pass. ### M4 quantity reconciliation — verified local checkpoint

Published normally to GitHub main at `52d4f72ce8b9bca1b53bae875d874af22817e1fe`; the remote commit, tree and parent match the reviewed checkpoint and the post-push authoritative checkout was clean. Current branch rules permitted the push. Exact [focused CI 37138214140](https://github.com/natannielz/Capstone/actions/runs/37138214140) completed successfully. Source/publication proofs are `tmp/production/ocr-results/source-reconciliation-checkpoint-release1.json` and `source-reconciliation-publication-release1.json`. This is source publication, not deployment acceptance.

The operator workflow compares an explicit named manual observation against the complete genuine quantity baseline of a stopped, reviewed Unit Toko WebLedger pool, then records an independent review. Backend build `source-reconciliation-release7` passes with zero warnings/errors. Compiled and generated contract checks pass; existing routes are unchanged, with the exact new warehouse purpose added to its existing enum. Focused frontend checks `source-reconciliation-frontend-focused-release4`, changed-file lint and production build `source-reconciliation-frontend-release2` pass, with build ID `pTuMFiOofPo0aMQNQ7aRS`. See the [operator runbook](../runbooks/stock-source-reconciliation.md).

The candidate preserves all-channel scope, genuine lot/receipt ownership, current approved policy, exact quantities, explicit discrepancies and original-key recovery. The independent reviewer enters blank observation fields; confirmation cannot refresh the maker's original timestamp. Named source discovery remains separate from baseline eligibility, so an ineligible pool does not prevent historical access. Existing general count observations and source-policy procedure references do not stand in for performed reconciliation. No external acknowledgment, monetary reconciliation, source activation, physical release, staging or production acceptance is supplied. Real source-owner/control inputs, producer-complete activation, other source modes and the remaining import/reconciliation scope stay open.

The first core run caught a shared integration failure: new outbox rows omitted the existing worker envelope's `status`, `attempts` and `dueAt`. Mongo validation rejected the rows and rolled back the commands. After three cases reached that same failure, root stopped only the verified owned test host; `source-reconciliation-core-release1.trx` is an aborted failed run, with remaining cases unverified. Its log and build provenance are preserved. The source correction uses the established envelope and leaves database validation strict. ### M2 authenticated recipient QR and exact receipt — 30 September 2026

- Surat Jalan checkpoint `d5140584bccf824ee570cbd5d7b4cd0b806b6888` is published on main; exact Focused quality run `36615401966` passed. The assigned courier can now select an eligible buyer/PIC, review the choice and create a QR bound to that recipient, shipment and issued document version/hash. The recipient opens `/fulfillment/receive`, authenticates in a separate session, declares every accepted/rejected/missing quantity and explicitly consents to a versioned Indonesian statement. Courier and recipient must be different verified people. Existing templates are unchanged; `delivery.evidence` and `receipt.accept` remain separately scoped permissions.
- Each line records whole packages plus a normalized loose remainder against the immutable dispatched package definition. Checked base-unit totals conserve the exact dispatch; discrepancies require reasons and remain visible on the recorded receipt. Recipient/profile, document hash, actual quantities, original courier/person references, consent and server time are preserved. Receipt, single-use consumption, audit, original command receipt and ID-only outbox commit together. This adds no stock or monetary effect and does not silently create an invoice, accept an after-sales case or issue a refund.
- QR secrets use 32 random bytes and are stored only as hashes, with 15-minute expiry, five failed-token attempts and bounded issuance per shipment/hour. Only the authenticated intended recipient can consume attempts. A QR is not acceptance. Raw secrets stay in memory/URL fragments and authenticated POST bodies; the fragment is cleared before asynchronous account work and never persisted for login/recovery. Lookup-only recovery cannot reconstruct a secret, resubmit or close an unknown operation. An explicit new QR after reconciliation is distinct from recreating a committed receipt. Current membership, grants, scope, session, independent-person proof and expiry are fenced through completion.
- Forward migration 26 installs the two receipt collections, strict bounded schemas, original-issue and shipment uniqueness, and the shipment/time range index used for issuance limits. It preflights both collections and reverse effects before DDL, preserves compatible stronger empty validators and safe counters, and leaves migrations 1–25 unchanged. All nine final migration checks passed in `shipment-receipt-migration-final.trx`, including the range-index preflight and preserved predecessor history. See the [receipt migration/restore runbook](../runbooks/shipment-receipt-migration.md). No actual production migration occurred.

The old dispatch assertion was corrected to wait for actual asynchronously loaded receipt state.
- `shipment-receipt-browser-final.trx` passes all three actual normal-runtime cases without skips: mobile receipt journey, existing dispatch/document journey, and recipient HTTP/account-discovery boundaries. The receipt browser uses production Next, HTTPS, Kestrel and real isolated Mongo; it creates a rendered QR, denies the courier's recipient screen, requires explicit quantities and unchecked consent, verifies offline submission is disabled, loses the committed response, reloads and recovers one original receipt without resending. Source, whole inspection, current pool version and approved procedure are frozen before consent. One immutable request, conditional pool head, actual-person command receipt, audit and metadata outbox commit together. Competitors cannot overwrite or double-claim the pool. Missing request/head/source/effect history is unavailable rather than empty capacity; actual prerequisite timestamps are verified even when a forged event has internally consistent hashes/effects.

The status is `financial_review_requested`: original-line attribution is held for independent review. Issued invoices, paid/credited amounts, available/refund-reserved receipt funds, original bank cash/journals and quarantine stock remain intact. This maker stage does not approve a credit, journal an entitlement, reverse an allocation, reserve cash, pay out or close a complaint. The next approval must consume the exact stored request while atomically posting the approved credit, any required canonical allocation release and refund reservation; no spendable interval may appear between those effects. Rejection/amendment/withdrawal must preserve attribution history; these commands, independently verified payout and the remaining source obligations remain active.

The actual browser uses separate financial request controls for an existing credit/refund proposal. Nonzero issued tax/fees require explicit input; zero issued components can be prefilled with exact zero. Exact owner/action/state/quantities/component totals/version are checked before a committed result can be accepted.

- Provider: maintained ASP.NET Core OIDC code + PKCE with exact issuer/audience/nonce/state/signature checks, bounded allowlisted returns and separate primary-authentication and actual OTP completion times. Versioned two-stage Action drafts bind a newly completed OTP to the current server challenge. Login state lasts ten minutes; step-up state lasts five.
- Sessions: random browser reference, hashed Mongo key, purpose-separated encrypted provider payload, 30-minute idle and original eight-hour absolute deadlines, shared certificate-protected key-store construction, bound one-use step-up rotation and family logout. Every prepared production actor includes its session/version/security epoch. Existing pure domain fixtures retain optional session references.
- Transactions: callback admission, profile/epoch initialization, invitation redemption, session creation/rotation and state consumption share one Mongo transaction; the cookie is written after commit. Business writes recheck/fence the exact identity and persistent session. Real pending-write tests cover logout, recovery and rotation.
- Admission: no open signup or email-based account merging. Invitations reserve stable IDs and bind intended email, provider/connection, scope and materialized role. A separate scoped approver must possess the complete delegated authority. Redemption rechecks both requester and approver revisions/current memberships/scopes without inventing fresh MFA for an earlier approval. Wrong, expired, replayed, revoked or reconfigured-provider invitations do not grant access.
- Administration: scoped verified-profile/invitation listings filter before count/page and record purpose-limited reads. Rejection, withdrawal and revocation are versioned and idempotent. Audit snapshots exclude email, invitation secrets, challenge nonces and encrypted provider payloads.
- Recovery: the prepared service increments the security epoch and revokes local sessions before persisting a blocked encrypted provider intention in the same transaction. It accepts no replacement password/factor secret and does not reactivate accounts. Provider delivery, the owner's completion flow and pending-recovery sign-in policy remain activation requirements.
- Migration v4: explicit/resumable strict identity schemas and indexes, preserved earlier validators/history, exact Int64 references, reserved-ID high-water handling, session/state TTL cleanup and no invented account/profile/approval seeds. Existing identities may lack a security epoch until verified approved admission initializes it; no production session accepts an absent epoch.

Verification: the full backend suite passes **234/234**, zero skipped, including **66 identity cases**; the versioned Action fixtures pass **5/5**. The backend compiled successfully, server OpenAPI export matches, generated frontend types match, and the diff check passes. The new Action tests are included in the quality workflow. No frontend source changed in this slice, so the prior frontend acceptance remains its baseline; no new browser/UI acceptance is claimed. Hosted CI has not run.

## ACC01.3 local provider-event checkpoint

Implemented native Auth0 CloudEvent and supplemental JSONARRAY log receivers with separate rotating bearer credentials, configured issuer/tenant/stream binding, trusted HTTPS, bounded parsing and strict machine/browser isolation. Both default to disabled. Three private v5 collections record durable deduplication, known-account holds and a shared provider-subject fence without creating an account from a provider message.

Recognized events advance account/session authority atomically. Block/delete/factor loss restrict even an initially unknown subject racing first invitation admission. Password/factor changes set a monotonic received-time cutoff; neither an old primary login nor old factor proof can restore access. Old events/replay cannot clear restrictions or lower revisions. ### Production-branch validation queue — 29 September 2026

The Quality workflow now preserves an in-progress run on codex/unit-toko-production when a newer checkpoint is pushed. The existing branch-specific concurrency group queues the newest pending revision; a later push can replace a pending revision, but does not cancel the running full regression. Other branches and pull requests retain their earlier cancellation behavior. Test jobs, coverage, the six-browser execution requirement and timeouts are unchanged.

This removes the need to hold verified local checkpoints solely to protect a long database run. Each published commit still needs its own exact CI outcome before that commit can be called fully CI-verified. A queued or superseded run is not a pass. The behavior follows [GitHub's documented conditional cancellation and default pending queue](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/write-workflows/choose-when-workflows-run/control-workflow-concurrency).
